Order: L.0, L.1, L.2, L.3, L.4, L.5, L.6, L.7, L.8, then L.9, L.10 (the console needs only L.1's
generated types and can be written beside L.2 to L.7), L.11. No step touches
`internal/adapters/raftmeta`; `test/integration` gets one new file: milestone 8 (8.0.10 onward)
runs beside it.

## Goal M — a bucket's details on its page, and "Delete all" behind the password (Albie, 2026-10-15)

"Add bucket details, e.g. number of objects, total storage used etc. Then add a delete all
button next to Delete selected; when the user clicks on it the system must ask for the user's
password before it deletes all the objects. When there is more than one object to delete,
use several at once to speed up the deletion."

| ID | Status | Step | Acceptance criterion | Files |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M.1 | todo | The details of a bucket at the top of its page | under the bucket's name: objects, storage used (with the unit the bucket grid uses), versions and delete markers where the bucket keeps versions, uploads in progress, versioning and object lock, created and by whom, the policy attached or "none", the last upload and download and who made them - all from what the API already answers (`GET /buckets?details=true` for the bucket, `GET /buckets/{bucket}/usage`, `GET /buckets/{bucket}/object/access` is not needed); a figure the caller may not see, or one the API says is not exact (a count beyond `MaxUsageScan`), is said so, never shown as 0; read again when an upload into it or a deletion ends; a phone | `web/src/screens/ObjectsScreen.tsx` (or a new `BucketDetails.tsx`), `web/src/styles/console.css` |
| M.2 | todo | "Delete all" asks for the password, and the server judges it | a button "Delete all" beside "Delete selected" (absent when the bucket is empty, disabled while a deletion runs); it opens a dialog that says what will happen in words that cannot be mistaken - every object of the bucket, how many, that a bucket keeping versions keeps them and gets delete markers, that versions under Object Lock are not removed - and asks for the password of the signed-in user (no user name); the password is checked by the SERVER through `POST /api/v1/session/unlock` (Goal L: re-authentication of an active session, same Argon2id path, throttle and audit, rotating the session and CSRF tokens) before the first deletion is sent; a wrong password (403), throttling (429) and an ended session (401) say so and delete nothing; the password is never kept, logged or put in a notice; an API key caller never sees the button | `web/src/screens/ObjectsScreen.tsx`, `web/src/api/client.ts` |
| M.3 | todo | Several deletions at once | the deletions of "Delete all" and of "Delete selected" go at most 8 at once (one limiter, shared by both, not shared with uploads); every page of the listing is walked, not only the one shown (keys are listed in pages of 1000 and deleted as they come; a key added meanwhile may be left and is said so); each deletion is still one `DELETE` through the generated client and judged by the authorizer for its object; a refusal (Object Lock, no right) is counted and its reason kept, the rest go on; progress in the activity bar as for uploads ("Deleting 1 200 of 5 000"), cancel stops what has not begun; the outcome a notice: deleted, not deleted and why; it goes on when another screen is opened, as uploads do (held by the console, not the screen); leaving the page asks first | `web/src/deletions/` (new, beside `web/src/uploads/`), `web/src/ui/ActivityBar.tsx`, `web/src/screens/ObjectsScreen.tsx` |
| M.4 | todo | Tests, review, gates, roll-out | console tests of every criterion (incl. the server's 403/429/401 at the password, refusals mid-way, pages beyond the first, the limit of 8 never passed, cancel, navigation); reviewer findings met; `make check` green; seen in a browser at a desktop and a phone width; deployed to k3s with Goal L | - |

Order: after Goal L is committed (M.2 needs its re-authentication). No change of the API or of the
replicated state is planned: if the reviewer rules that "every object after a password" must be one
server operation rather than many judged deletions, that is a contract change (an ADR, design-first).

## Milestone 9 — Visual policy builder (Albie, 2026-10-13)

"Add the visual policy builder to the milestones." The console has no editor of policies
that is not a box of JSON, and none at all for a bucket's policy. This milestone gives it
one that is built by choosing - who, what, where, when - and shows the JSON it comes to;
the JSON stays the truth, and whatever decides is still the one `ports.Authorizer`.

| ID | Status | Step | Acceptance criterion | Files |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 9.0 | todo | Plan (planner) and an ADR if the contract grows | this table made into steps that can each be gated; what the builder covers and what it leaves to the JSON view said plainly (every Effect, Action, Resource and Condition the product decides by; `NotAction`/`NotResource`/`Principal` forms as they exist); open questions for Albie with their defaults | `PLAN.md`, `docs/adr/` |
| 9.1 | todo | Contract first: what the builder needs from the API | `api/openapi/admin.yaml`: the catalogue of actions (`s3:*`, `admin:*`) with what each allows, the condition keys and their operators, served by the node (so a newer node's actions are offered and none is invented in React); validation of a policy document without storing it, answering every fault with where it is; additive, `oasdiff` clean | `api/openapi/admin.yaml`, `internal/adapters/adminapi/`, `internal/app/`, `internal/domain/iam/` |
| 9.2 | todo | The builder for IAM policies | a policy is made and changed by statements: effect, actions chosen from the catalogue by group with search, resources by bucket and prefix with the buckets that exist offered, conditions by key, operator and value; the JSON beside it follows every change and a change of the JSON is read back into the builder, and what the builder cannot show is kept and marked, never dropped; nothing is stored that the API's validation refuses; keyboard and screen reader; a phone | `web/src/screens/`, `web/src/ui/` |
| 9.3 | todo | The same for a bucket's policy | a bucket's policy is read, made, changed and removed in the console with the builder (principals as the product has them - a user, a group, a role, anyone), with a plain warning before a statement that lets anyone read or write; S3 `PutBucketPolicy` and the console come to the same stored document | `web/src/screens/BucketsScreen.tsx`, `api/openapi/admin.yaml`, `internal/adapters/adminapi/` |
| 9.4 | todo | "Who may do this?" | for a chosen user or key, an action and a resource, the answer of the `ports.Authorizer` itself - allowed or not, and by which statement of which policy (or by none: default deny) - through an endpoint of its own with its own action, audited; no decision is reckoned in React | `api/openapi/admin.yaml`, `internal/app/authz.go`, `web/src/screens/` |
| 9.5 | todo | Tests, review, documents, roll-out | use-case and handler tests; console tests of every criterion; fuzz of the reading of a policy back into the builder (round trip: JSON to builder to JSON is the same document or is marked as kept as it is); reviewer findings met; `make check` green; README and ARCHITECTURE; the §9 report | - |

## Decisions

| Date | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-07 | Go 1.27.1 pinned in `go.mod`; builder image runs with `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`. | Latest stable on this date; the builder must not silently fetch another toolchain. |
| 2026-10-07 | One `Dockerfile` holds the product stages (`web`, `build`, final) **and** the `builder` tool stage. | One place pins the Go and Node images for both the product and the tools `make` runs with. |
| 2026-10-07 | Go tools `gofumpt`, `goimports`, `govulncheck`, `go-licenses` are `tool` directives in `go.mod`. `golangci-lint`, `helm`, the Docker CLI, Node and pnpm are pinned by digest/version in the `builder` stage. | §2 asks for `tool` directives. golangci-lint's dependency graph is too large to share one `go.mod` with the product, and the others are not Go tools. None of them is needed for `go build`. |
| 2026-10-07 | The binary has no third-party Go dependency in milestone 0 (`flag`, `net/http`, `log/slog`). | §5 "prefer the standard library". |
| 2026-10-07 | `/healthz` and `/readyz` live in a new inbound adapter `internal/adapters/health`, mounted on the admin listener (`:9001`). | They are operational endpoints, not part of the versioned, generated admin API. |
| 2026-10-07 | Probe endpoints are unauthenticated and return only `{"status":"ok"\|"unavailable"}`; failure detail goes to the log. | Orchestrator probes cannot hold credentials. Stricter alternative (authenticated probes) is an open question for Albie. |
| 2026-10-07 | `server` refuses to start without `--tls-cert/--tls-key` unless `--dev-plaintext` is set; plaintext accepts only a loopback **IP literal** (no host names). | §3 encryption in transit, stricter interpretation. |
| 2026-10-07 | TLS minimum is 1.2 with Go's default suites; 1.3 is negotiated when the client supports it. | §3. |
| 2026-10-07 | `make verify-clone` exports the working tree through a temporary index and object directory (`git add -A` → `git write-tree` → `git archive`), not `HEAD`. | The gate must be able to pass *before* the commit it guards, and must not write into `.git`. |
| 2026-10-07 | `make image` builds one local image per architecture (`cactusfs:<v>-amd64`, `-arm64`) and checks each is < 60 MB. A pushed manifest list is step 7.3. | A multi-arch manifest cannot be loaded into every local image store; per-arch builds work unchanged on Docker and Podman. |
| 2026-10-07 | Console scaffold exists in milestone 0 but is not embedded or served until 5.4. | The `Dockerfile` needs the Node stage and the frontend gate needs a subject; the goal limits `server` to the two probe endpoints. |
| 2026-10-07 | TypeScript 6.0.x rather than 7.x. | `typescript-eslint` 8 supports `< 6.1`. |
| 2026-10-07 | No `LICENSE` file. | Project licence is still open (§11). |
| 2026-10-07 | `--drain-delay` (default `0s`) was pulled forward from 7.6 into 0.3. | Review finding: without a delay the listener closes at once and the `/readyz` 503 can never be observed, so the 0.3 criterion was untestable. Default stays `0s` so local runs stop immediately. |
| 2026-10-07 | TLS 1.2 is limited to ECDHE suites with AES-GCM or ChaCha20-Poly1305. | Go's default list also accepts CBC-SHA1 suites; §3 stricter interpretation. |
| 2026-10-07 | `.golangci.yml` excludes gosec G204 ("subprocess launched with a variable") under `test/` only. No other exclusion and no `//nolint` exists. | The black-box tests exist to execute the binary they have just built; the finding cannot be fixed without deleting the test. Listed as an open question for Albie because it is a silenced finding. |
| 2026-10-07 | `make lint` runs `scripts/no-bypass.sh`, which rejects `//nolint`, `t.Skip*` and `testing.Short` in Go sources. | §10 as a gate rather than a convention. |
| 2026-10-07 | The licence gate checks the Go dependencies of all six release platforms and walks the console's production and optional dependencies. | A build-tagged dependency for Windows or macOS would otherwise ship unchecked. |
| 2026-10-07 | The console placeholder shows `cactusfs-logo.svg` and links `favicon.svg`; brand files are committed as supplied. | "Brand assets" rule added to `CLAUDE.md` §2 during this milestone. |
| 2026-10-07 | M1 dependencies: `hashicorp/raft` v1.8.0 and `raft-boltdb/v2` v2.4.2 (MPL-2.0), `go.etcd.io/bbolt` v1.5.0 (MIT), `golang.org/x/crypto` (BSD-3, Argon2id), `golang.org/x/sys` (BSD-3, Windows file lock), `prometheus/client_golang` v1.24.1 and OpenTelemetry v1.47.0 (Apache-2.0); test-only `aws-sdk-go-v2` (Apache-2.0). | Raft, bbolt, Prometheus and OTel are named by `CLAUDE.md`; Argon2id and the Windows lock have no standard-library equivalent. Planner verified licences and six-platform `CGO_ENABLED=0` builds. |
| 2026-10-07 | `ports.EventPublisher` is not introduced in M1; it arrives in 3.6 with its AsyncAPI contract. `IDGenerator` is replaced by an injected `io.Reader` entropy source. | Contract-first rule for events; fewer ports with one implementation. |
| 2026-10-07 | Stored-object metadata is erasure-shaped from the start: `Layout{DataShards:1, ParityShards:0, StripeSize, StoredSize, Shards[{DriveID, Size}]}`. Shard files carry a checksummed header and a SHA-256 per block bound to data ID, shard index and block number. | So that milestone 2 (erasure coding, heal, scrub) changes values, not the on-disk format or the Raft entry schema. |
| 2026-10-07 | Stream AAD is `index ‖ final ‖ caller AAD` (fixed-width fields first); caller AAD and the KMS context are the data ID. | Unambiguous layout; ciphertext and wrapped key cannot be moved to another object. |
| 2026-10-07 | Raft runs with `NoSnapshotRestoreOnStart`; the FSM keeps its state in its own bbolt file and stores the last applied index in the same transaction as each change. | The state DB is already durable: restoring an older snapshot over it on every start would be wrong, and replay must be idempotent. |
| 2026-10-07 | `STREAMING-UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD-TRAILER` (aws-chunked with trailing checksum) is implemented in 1.8, not 6.3. Signed streaming and presigned URLs stay in 6.3. | It is what current AWS SDKs send by default over TLS, and the server is TLS-only. |
| 2026-10-07 | ETag is the MD5 of the object, computed in one file of the S3 adapter (`internal/adapters/s3api/md5.go`); `.golangci.yml` excludes gosec G401/G501 for that file only. | S3 clients compare ETag and `Content-MD5` with MD5; it is a protocol checksum here, not a security primitive (integrity is AES-GCM plus SHA-256). A silenced finding: open question for Albie. |
| 2026-10-07 | `/metrics` requires `Authorization: Bearer <access-key-id>:<secret>` and passes `ports.Authorizer` (`admin:GetMetrics`). | §3 stricter reading: every inbound request is authenticated. Prometheus can send a bearer credential. The token format is provisional until the admin API contract (5.1). |
| 2026-10-07 | Generated root credentials are printed once to stderr, never to the JSON log and never to a file. | "Shown once"; a file would be a secret stored unencrypted. Container log drivers capture stderr, so production should supply the credentials: open question for Albie. |
| 2026-10-07 | Authentication failures and denials are logged (without secrets) until the hash-chained audit log exists (4.6). | Audit trail is milestone 4. |
| 2026-10-07 | In M1, shards of failed, replaced and deleted objects are deleted best-effort after the fact; a crash can leave orphans until the collector of 2.7. | Orphans waste space but are never visible: the Raft commit is the only visibility switch. |
| 2026-10-07 | govet's optional `shadow` analyzer is disabled in `.golangci.yml` (all other optional analyzers stay on, except `fieldalignment`). | It is not part of `go vet` and rejects the idiomatic `if v, err := f(); err != nil` scoped error. It flagged exactly that idiom twice in test code the first time `enable-all` met real code. |
| 2026-10-07 | Secrets (KMS passphrase, root credentials) are read from the environment or a file only; they have no command-line flag. | A command line is visible to every user of the machine. This narrows "matching flags" in §2 for secrets. |
| 2026-10-07 | A missing local KMS key file is created only when the metadata directory is unused; on an existing deployment it is an error. | Creating a new key at a mistyped path would leave every object unreadable while the node looked healthy. |
| 2026-10-07 | The S3 listener has header and idle timeouts but no whole-request or whole-response timeout; the admin listener bounds both. | An object transfer may legitimately take hours. Slow-body abuse of the S3 listener is an open risk until per-read deadlines are added (threat model). |
| 2026-10-07 | S3 operations not implemented yet answer `501 NotImplemented` after authentication (ACLs, versioning, multipart, tagging, copy, multi-delete, ListObjects v1, presigned and signed-streaming requests); a recognised sub-resource is never treated as the plain operation on the same path. | A `DELETE /key?uploadId=…` served as DeleteObject would destroy data. |
| 2026-10-07 | SHA-1 trailing or header checksums are refused (`InvalidRequest`); CRC32, CRC32C, CRC64NVME and SHA-256 are verified. | gosec rejects SHA-1, and adding a second weak-hash exclusion is not justified while SDK defaults are CRC-based. |
| 2026-10-07 | SigV4 test vectors from the AWS documentation live in `internal/adapters/s3api/testdata/sigv4-examples.json`. | They contain AWS's public example key pair, which secret scanners flag when written in source. |
| 2026-10-07 | The compatibility suite builds and starts the real binary over TLS with a generated CA, and configures the SDK's default checksum behaviour explicitly instead of loading AWS configuration. | `cmd/cactusfs` is the only wiring point; the suite must not read the developer's AWS files; and a hand-built `aws.Config` otherwise silently turns the SDK's default checksums off (found when a test that should have exercised aws-chunked did not). |
| 2026-10-07 | `--otlp-endpoint` must be `https`, or `http` to a loopback IP. | Encryption in transit also applies to what the node sends out. |
| 2026-10-07 | Bucket names, object keys and object metadata are not encrypted in the metadata store. | §3 requires object encryption; encrypting the index is a product decision with a listing-performance cost. Open question for Albie. |
| 2026-10-07 | Review of milestone 1 (2 blocker, 5 major, 6 minor) — all fixed, each with a regression test that fails when the fix is removed. | Recorded so the reasoning survives: see the next rows. |
| 2026-10-07 | The stream encryptor ends the plaintext only on `io.EOF`; `io.ErrUnexpectedEOF` from the source is an error (`IncompleteBody`). | Blocker: a chunked or length-declared upload cut short by the client was sealed as final and committed, replacing the existing object. |
| 2026-10-07 | `domain.ErrOutcomeUnknown`: an error from the metadata store is a definite "not committed" only for state-machine refusals and errors Raft returns before logging. Otherwise the entry may still be applied, and nothing it refers to is cleaned up. The state machine is fail-stop: once an entry cannot be applied, no further entry is logged. | Blocker: `Put` deleted the new shard on any commit error, although the entry could be applied at the next start — leaving the index pointing at no data and, for an overwrite, the previous version unreferenced. |
| 2026-10-07 | S3 routing is an allow-list: a query parameter or `x-amz-*` header the routed operation does not know answers `501`; a query string that does not parse answers `400`; conditional headers (`If-Match`, `If-None-Match`, `If-Modified-Since`, `If-Unmodified-Since`) answer `501` until implemented (6.x). | Major: the deny-list missed new sub-resources, `;` in a query dropped the pair, and `If-None-Match: *` or object-lock headers were silently ignored. |
| 2026-10-07 | `Get` looks again when the shard of the version it read is gone and the object has since changed. | Major: a read racing an overwrite or delete failed with 500. |
| 2026-10-07 | At start the metadata store restores the newest snapshot when the state database is older than it. | Major: with `NoSnapshotRestoreOnStart`, a lost or stale `state.db` came up healthy with only the post-snapshot log applied. |
| 2026-10-07 | `serve` returns only when every request handler has returned; the metadata store refuses use after `Close` and `Close` waits for operations in flight. If handlers do not return within 60 s the process exits without closing the stores. | Major: after a shutdown timeout the stores were closed under running handlers. |
| 2026-10-07 | The KMS key file and `node.json` are created with their directory entry flushed (`internal/adapters/fsutil`). | Major: a power loss after first start could lose the key file while acknowledged objects existed. |
| 2026-10-07 | `Content-MD5` must be covered by the signature when present; the CreateBucket body is verified against its declared hash; a shard whose rename could not be made durable is removed again; the local KMS key ID format is validated. | Minor review findings. |
| 2026-10-07 | M2 dependency: `github.com/klauspost/reedsolomon` v1.14.2 (MIT; brings `klauspost/cpuid/v2`, MIT). Pure Go, builds for all six targets. | Named by `CLAUDE.md` §2. |
| 2026-10-07 | Erasure sets must be of equal size: the number of drives is divided into the fewest sets of at most 16; a count that does not divide evenly (17, 19, …) is refused at start. | Unequal sets would have different durability; refusing is explicit. |
| 2026-10-07 | Drives are bound to slots (set, position) in Raft metadata by drive ID at first start. An object's layout names drive IDs. All drives must open at start. (Automatic adoption of an empty drive, decided here first, was withdrawn after review: see below.) | Addressing by ID makes `--data` order irrelevant. Starting with a drive offline is deferred (risk: a dead drive needs replacing before restart). |
| 2026-10-07 | Review of milestone 2 (3 blocker, 5 major, 4 minor) — all fixed, each with a regression test that fails when the fix is removed; three of the blockers are also reproduced as chaos scenarios. ADR-0004 was revised in place before the milestone closed. | Recorded so the reasoning survives: see the next rows. |
| 2026-10-07 | After healing, shards are removed only when a lookup succeeds and shows the version replaced or gone, and only the shards that heal rebuilt. A failed lookup removes nothing. | Blocker: a lookup cancelled by shutdown was read as "replaced" and every shard of a healthy object was deleted. |
| 2026-10-07 | A deployment has a random ID; every drive is claimed for it in `cactusfs-drive.json` (`cluster_id`, reserved by ADR-0001). A new deployment needs drives that are all empty and unclaimed. | Blocker: with an empty metadata directory, drives full of data were adopted as a new deployment and the collector deleted every shard. |
| 2026-10-07 | Replacing a drive needs parity and the operator's confirmation for that start (`--allow-drive-replacement`); without parity it is never done. "Empty" is read from the drive (no shard file), not from having formatted it. | Blocker: an unmounted mount point looked like a new drive, took the slot and retired the real drive for good, even on a one-drive node. Major: a start interrupted after formatting left a deployment that could not start. |
| 2026-10-07 | Healing rebuilds a shard only on definite damage (`ErrShardNotFound`, `ErrIntegrity`) and through `ShardStore.Rewrite`, which replaces the old content in one rename. One heal per object at a time. | Major: any open or read error was treated as damage and the shard deleted before a replacement existed. |
| 2026-10-07 | Writes pin their data ID until the commit outcome is definite (unknown outcomes for the life of the process); the collector skips pinned data and does nothing while the metadata is not ready; `IsDataReferenced` fails on a stopped state machine. Minimum `--gc-grace` is 1 h. | Major: the grace period alone stood between a slow upload, or an unapplied log entry, and the collector. |
| 2026-10-07 | The scrubber is throttled as it reads and counts only verified bytes. A shard is still given up for the rest of a read after one bad block (ADR-0004, consequences). | Minor review findings; the second is a recorded limitation, not fixed. |
| 2026-10-07 | Milestone 3. Every node runs Raft: `meta` nodes vote, the others are non-voters with a local replica. Changes are forwarded to the leader; reads are linearizable on every node by read index. ADR-0005. | Local reads and listings on every node, one code path for a node alone and a cluster. Reads CLAUDE.md's "stateless gateway" as "holds nothing that cannot be rebuilt" — **open question for Albie**; the alternative is gateways that ask a voter for every metadata read. |
| 2026-10-07 | The leader proves leadership by asking the voters for their Raft term (majority, itself included), not with `raft.VerifyLeader`; reads wait on the state machine's own applied command index, not `raft.AppliedIndex`. | Found by the multi-node tests: `VerifyLeader` counts answers of non-voters, so a leader cut off from the other voters but not from a storage node would pass and serve stale reads; `AppliedIndex` advances before the state machine has applied. |
| 2026-10-07 | A node's identity is its certificate: the node ID is the first DNS name, clients verify it as `ServerName` whatever the address, Raft members are recorded as `<id>@<host:port>`. The node CA signs node certificates only. No plaintext node RPC or Raft, in development either. | Addresses and DNS are not trusted to say who is behind them; stricter reading of §3 mTLS. **Open question:** certificates are read at start (rotation = restart) and there is no revocation. |
| 2026-10-07 | Every node RPC response carries an `Error`; a failed RPC means "not reached or no answer". Domain sentinels cross nodes as stable names (`domain.Codes`). | The write path must tell "definitely not committed" from "outcome unknown"; only an answer is definite. |
| 2026-10-07 | A cluster forms once, from `--peers` (the complete node list): voters bootstrap with the identical member list when every node has answered and none knows a deployment; the leader then surveys all drives and records the topology. 3 or 5 voters and at least 4 storage nodes are enforced. Storage cannot be added to a formed cluster yet. | Identical bootstrap needs every node's self-description; a deployment ID anywhere means "join, never form". Expansion needs new erasure sets and rebalancing — its own milestone. |
| 2026-10-07 | Topology slots carry their node; a node reconciles only its own drives at start; an unreachable drive is `ErrDriveOffline` — not damage, not a reason to rewrite or delete; requests that fail only for lack of reachable nodes answer 503. | A node that is down must not keep the others from starting, and must never be mistaken for lost data. |
| 2026-10-07 | Scrubbing runs on the metadata leader only; garbage collection on every storage node for its own drives, asking every recorded node for writes in progress (unreachable = yes), and looking at the index again afterwards. | One scrubber for the cluster; the second look at the index closes the gap in which a write commits and unpins between the two questions (the order in milestone 2 had that gap behind the grace period). |
| 2026-10-07 | `MetadataVersion` per node; nobody writes an entry format newer than the oldest member's; the leader re-checks. | CLAUDE.md §2: version N must run alongside N+1. Tested with a stand-in for release N+1. |
| 2026-10-07 | Events: derived in the state machine after commit, IDs from the entry index; in-process publisher is bounded, never blocks, at most once per node. AsyncAPI validated with `@asyncapi/specs` + `ajv` (dev dependencies of `web/`), not `@asyncapi/parser`. | The state machine must never wait for a consumer. The parser pulls in ~160 packages and an install-time analytics package; the schema, `ajv` and a `$ref` check do the job in 9. |
| 2026-10-07 | New dependencies: `google.golang.org/grpc` and `protobuf` (already linked through the OTLP exporter; now direct), `protoc-gen-go` and `protoc-gen-go-grpc` as `tool` directives, `buf` pinned by digest in the builder, `testcontainers-go` (tests only; not linked into the binary). | Node RPC contract and its breaking-change gate; real multi-node integration and chaos tests. |
| 2026-10-07 | Until the Vault adapter (4.4), the nodes of a cluster share one sealed KMS key file; a cluster member never creates a key file. `cactusfs dev-init` writes it with throw-away certificates for development, and never overwrites. | Every node must unwrap every object's key. Creating a key per node would make objects unreadable through other nodes. |
| 2026-10-07 | The image ships `/var/lib/cactusfs/{data1..4,meta,config}` owned by UID 65532. | A named volume takes the ownership of the directory it is first mounted on; without them the non-root process cannot write to its volumes. |
| 2026-10-07 | In a cluster `CommitTimeout` is 10 ms; listeners (probes included) still open only after a node has joined its cluster. | Followers learn of commits with the leader's next message, and every read on a non-leader waits for that. Early probes are needed for Helm and moved to 7.1. |
| 2026-10-07 | Review of milestone 3 (2 blocker, 3 major, 9 minor, 2 nit). Fixed: a topology recorded before slots named their node is migrated at first start (upgrade test, and proven with the previous commit's binary); PLAN statuses; the Raft port admits voters only and completes its handshake with a deadline; only a node may propose its own record; the leader decides who votes and the voters are fixed at formation; tests that pin leadership confirmation by voters and a new leader's catch-up (each kills the mutant the reviewer found alive); node certificates carry exactly one name; the state machine validates a node record only by rules every release shares; generated root credentials are shown even when their commit was uncertain; `noderpc` no longer imports `nodetls`; compose and README wording. | Recorded so the reasoning survives. |
| 2026-10-07 | Deferred from that review, with reasons: any node certificate may still propose bucket, object and topology changes (narrowing by role belongs to IAM, milestone 4; stated in ADR-0005 and the threat model); the dev compose cluster shares one config volume, so nodes can read each other's keys (stated in the file); three tests assert "nothing happens" with short fixed sleeps; a certificate with two names is refused by code that no test exercises with such a certificate. | Honest list of what was not done. |
| 2026-10-08 | Milestone 4. A request's identity - key, owner, every policy that applies - is resolved in one linearizable read at authentication and travels in the principal; `iam.Decide` is the only place a decision is made. ADR-0006. | One read-index round per request; revocation, disabling and role changes take effect with the next request on every node. |
| 2026-10-08 | Policy parsing is strict (unknown, repeated or differently-cased fields, unknown operators and keys, empty conditions are errors). Conditions on an unknown source address are decided against the request in both Allow and Deny. | A policy must mean what it reads; the fuzzers and the review each found a way it did not (a literal `*` in a key; duplicate fields; a Deny that failed open). |
| 2026-10-08 | Built-in roles and policies are part of the binary, not of the state; the proposer vouches for built-in names in the entry so that the state machine decides nothing a release could decide differently. | Same meaning everywhere, extendable by upgrade, no divergence between replicas of two releases. |
| 2026-10-08 | IAM, session and audit commands are added to entry schema version 1, not a version 2. | No release has shipped version 1. A released schema would need a new version and the rolling-upgrade rule of ADR-0005. |
| 2026-10-08 | Changes to identities carry their audit record in the same Raft entry. Refusals and other administrative changes are appended in the background; refusals are coalesced by what the caller cannot vary and capped per window; under flood a record per refusal can be dropped (counted, logged), a change never. | Atomic where it matters most; an unauthenticated client must not be able to grow the replicated log one request at a time. **Open question for Albie:** CLAUDE.md §3 says every admin mutation, authentication failure and denial is an audit event - read strictly that allows no dropping at all, which would let a flood stop the store. |
| 2026-10-08 | Vault Transit through `net/http`, no Vault SDK; data keys are made on the node and wrapped with `encrypt` + associated data; a key type that cannot bind associated data is refused at start; token from a file, re-read at every use; no redirects, no proxy. | Smallest dependency and attack surface; the SDK is MPL-2.0 and large. Token renewal and other auth methods are left to the platform. |
| 2026-10-08 | Console passwords: Argon2id `m=65536,t=3,p=4` behind `ports.PasswordHasher` (the domain may not import `x/crypto`), at most four hashes at once. Sessions: 30 minutes, fixed, replicated, stored as hashes; `__Host-` cookie; CSRF token per session. | RFC 9106's second recommended set; the cap bounds memory when sign-in becomes reachable in milestone 5. No lockout or rate limit yet. |
| 2026-10-08 | The root credential is read once per process and stays the way back in: it has no policy and cannot be locked out. | It is written once and never changes; a policy mistake must not be unrecoverable. |
| 2026-10-08 | Review of milestone 4 (3 blocker, 6 major, 9 minor). Fixed: denials with long names were not audited; repeat counts lost at a window boundary; PLAN statuses; flooding the trail by varying the claimed key or the object; Deny conditions that failed open (unknown address, zoned IPv6, IPv4-mapped ranges); duplicate, differently-cased and empty fields accepted by the parser; audit records that did not say what was granted; bucket, bootstrap, topology and registration changes not audited; tests for owner-scoped key permissions, the Vault token's destination, the node's clock, literal wildcards. Also: last use only after the signature verified; narrowed keys cannot make keys; refusals do not name a key's owner; Vault's refusal (not an error on the way) proves the binding; Argon2id concurrency cap. | Recorded so the reasoning survives. |
| 2026-10-08 | Deferred from that review: `UpdateUser` without compare-and-set, and a password change not ending sessions; the built-in-immutable rule lives in the store adapters; no test that a failed `/metrics` authentication is audited; sign-out of a session that is already gone writes a record; the map of touched keys is never pruned. | Honest list of what was not done; the first is stated in ADR-0006. |
| 2026-10-08 | Raft replicates one request at a time per follower (`MaxRPCsInFlight: 1`, no pipelining), and a node that stops closes the Raft connections it accepted. | Found by a test that hung for 30 minutes: with pipelining, a follower that takes what it is sent and never answers blocks the leader's replication goroutine for good, and Raft's shutdown waits for that goroutine. Costs replication throughput, which metadata does not need; not benchmarked. |
| 2026-10-08 | Raft learns a node's advertised address from the stream layer's `Addr()`, not from an address provider that answered "use the configured address" for every other node. | Without pipelining that provider made Raft log a warning on every replication request, which drowned the node log (and hid the cause of a chaos failure). |

OpenAPI 3.0.3 (highest `oapi-codegen` 2.8.0 fully supports); strict generated server; which operations are public is read from the contract. `oasdiff` 1.33.0 comes into the builder image pinned by digest, not as a Go tool. | A Go `tool` directive for oasdiff would add its whole dependency tree to `go.mod`; the image is the same pinning with none of it. |
| 2026-10-08 | New dependencies: `github.com/oapi-codegen/oapi-codegen/v2` (tool, Apache-2.0), `github.com/oapi-codegen/runtime` (Apache-2.0, parameter binding of the generated server); in `web/`: `openapi-fetch` (MIT, runtime) and `openapi-typescript` (MIT, generator). | 2026-10-08 | `Idempotency-Key`: reserve, carry out, store the answer - two Raft entries around the change, answers sealed through the KMS, scoped to the caller, kept 24 h. Not atomic with the change. Four new commands in entry schema version 1. | The answer to creating a key holds a secret, so it may not be stored in the clear. Atomicity would put a record into every mutating entry; deferred, and it can be added compatibly. |
| 2026-10-08 | Objects stored through the management API get a SHA-256-derived entity tag marked `-1`; downloads are always attachments of an opaque type. | MD5 stays confined to the S3 adapter; an uploaded HTML file must never run on the console's origin. |
| 2026-10-08 | Sign-in attempts are limited per address and node, in memory (10 per 5 minutes). No per-user lockout. | A lockout by user name is a way to lock a user out on purpose. **Open question for Albie.** |
| 2026-10-08 | Sign-in, the contract, `/docs` and the console's files need no credential. | They cannot: nobody has one yet. **Open question for Albie** against "every inbound request is authenticated". |
| 2026-10-08 | Maintenance status is per answering node; the console is one bundle with a hash router and no dependencies beyond the generated client. | A cluster-wide view needs nodes to ask each other; left for later. |
| 2026-10-08 | Review of milestone 5 (0 blocker, 3 major, 9 minor). Fixed: uploads and downloads were recognised by a path ending in `/object`, so an entity of that name escaped the body limit, the media-type check and the request fingerprint - now by the matched operation; idempotency reservations were deleted by ID alone, so a slow request could undo the repeat that took over - the delete now names the reservation's time, and a reservation goes stale after 15 minutes, not 2; failed sign-ins were counted after the password check, so parallel guesses were not limited - now before, and the queue for password hashing is bounded. Also: HEAD of an object is 405; a test pins the exact set of public operations; tests for a user named `object`, bearer and cookie together, session and key scopes, CSRF token not in logs. | Recorded so the reasoning survives. |
| 2026-10-08 | Deferred from that review: sign-in limit keyed on the peer address only (shared behind a proxy; no trusted-proxy setting, no per-user count); parameters bound before authentication (400 instead of 401, unaudited); no cap on idempotency records per caller, and the console sends a fresh key on every change; upload fingerprint without content; no progress timeout on transfers; a new sign-in does not end the previous session; cookies shared between the S3 and admin ports. All in ADR-0007 under "Known limits". | Honest list of what was not done. |
| 2026-10-08 | Step 6.3. Presigned URLs (SigV4 in the query string, 1 second to 7 days) and signed aws-chunked uploads (`STREAMING-AWS4-HMAC-SHA256-PAYLOAD`, with and without signed trailers). A request with both a presigned query and an Authorization header is refused. Signed `x-amz-*` query parameters of a presigned URL are treated as the headers they stand for. A signed `content-length` header is taken from the request's length. Signature Version 2 and SigV4A stay unsupported. | Verified against the worked examples of the AWS documentation (presigned GET and the three chunk signatures) and with the AWS SDK's presigner in the compat suite. A presigned URL acts as its signer does at the time of use: revoking the key ends its URLs. |
| 2026-10-08 | Milestone 6 goes to Raft entry schema version 2 and state schema 2; this supersedes "extend version 1" for commands whose meaning or payload changes. | M4 and M5 only added command types, which an older binary stops on. M6 changes what `object.put`, `object.delete`, `bucket.create` and `bucket.delete` mean, and entries are decoded leniently: an older binary would drop a retention field and diverge silently. The version gate and the rolling-upgrade test already exist (ADR-0005). |
| 2026-10-08 | Versions are added beside the existing object index, not by re-keying it: no rewrite of stored data, but an ADR and an upgrade test, because existing records are reinterpreted (as null versions) and the rebuild of the shard reference index must walk the new bucket. | A rebuild that forgot noncurrent versions would let the collector delete their shards. |
| 2026-10-08 | Object Lock is decided in the state machine on a time the metadata leader stamps into the entry, kept monotonic by a high-water mark in the state. | App-level checks race across gateways, and replicas must agree. **Open question for Albie:** a voter with a fast clock that becomes leader ends retention early; the stricter option (minimum clock of a voter majority for time-relaxed deletes) is not built yet. |
| 2026-10-08 | Stricter readings chosen for milestone 6: `s3:BypassGovernanceRetention` is never matched by a wildcard; a bucket policy can grant anonymous access to read actions only; an SSE-C key is combined with a KMS data key, never used alone; security-relevant XML is parsed strictly; root is not bound by bucket policies. | CLAUDE.md §3: when ambiguous, the stricter interpretation, flagged. **Open questions for Albie**, each. |
| 2026-10-08 | Step 6.9 added (CopyObject, UploadPartCopy, ListObjects v1, conditional headers); DeleteObjects joins 6.2. | aws-cli and s3-tests in 6.8 use them. |
| 2026-10-08 | Step 6.2. Versions as designed in ADR-0008. The routing table and the object-extent refactor of 6.0 were not needed for it and move to the steps that need them (multipart). `ports.ObjectIndex.PutVersion` and `DeleteObject` return what was stored, removed and marked; `ObjectService.Delete` takes an input and returns an output. A GET of the current version whose newest version is a delete marker answers 404 without the `x-amz-delete-marker` header. | Smallest change that is right for SDK and CLI paths; the missing header is cosmetic and recorded here. |
| 2026-10-08 | Review of step 6.2 (0 blocker, 3 major, 11 minor; no sequence found that removes data a version refers to). Fixed: lookups and scans that cost time per version of a key, also inside the state machine - an index of versions by name, scans straight from the cursor that resume after a version; wrong answers once a member had not registered while a bucket keeps versions - the cluster's level no longer drops below what its state holds; version listings that lost keys with a delimiter ending in a non-ASCII byte. Also: strict XML that checks structure, not only names; unsigned and presigned bodies refused for DeleteObjects and PutBucketVersioning; a commit whose answer cannot be read is an unknown outcome, not a failure; events name null versions as clients do; `bucket.create` with schema 2 fields is a schema 2 entry; a version ID a key already has is refused (found by the new index check). | Recorded so the reasoning survives. |

| 2026-10-08 | The chaos failures of `TestClusterPartition` (milestone 4, and again after 6.2) were the test's, not the product's: after a container's network is taken away and given back, the engine (Docker Desktop) now and then reports a published port that nothing listens on, however often the network is reconnected; the node was running and had rejoined. The partition is now made inside the node's network namespace with routes to nowhere, which leaves the client's path alone, and the test requires that the isolated node answers the client with a 5xx - before, a refused connection passed for a refusal. `TestClusterWithTwoNodesDown` failed twice for a fault of ours: a 503 sent before the upload was read reached the client as a reset connection; for an authenticated caller up to 1 MiB of body is now read first. Four chaos runs in a row are green. | Found by making the failing test say what it saw instead of running it again. |
| 2026-10-08 | **Open risk.** Seen once each under the load of a full `make check` and not reproduced alone: a port taken between a test node's stop and restart (`TestClusterRestart`), and `TestClusterRefusesWhatItMustNotBecome/three_storage_nodes` waiting its full 60 seconds. The second is unexplained. A node RPC test with a 300 ms deadline was given 3 seconds. | Not resolved, and not to be read as resolved because later runs were green. |
| 2026-10-08 | Step 6.4, ADR-0009. Object Lock is enforced in the state machine on the leader's time; the rules are two functions in the domain with no parameter that lifts compliance or a legal hold. The default retention of a bucket lives in the bucket's record (`bucket.lock.put`), not in a general bucket-configuration store: that arrives with the step that needs it (6.7). GET and HEAD return a version's lock to whoever may read the version. | Smallest design that holds against root and against any proposer. **Open questions for Albie:** bypass never granted by a wildcard is stricter than AWS; lock headers on GET without `s3:GetObjectRetention` is laxer; the fast-clock limit of ADR-0008. |
| 2026-10-08 | Review of step 6.4 (0 blocker, 2 major, 7 minor; no request from any principal, root included, removes or weakens a compliance-locked or held version). Both majors are about a node that proposes arbitrary entries. Fixed: such a node could give a second version the data of a locked one and have an honest gateway remove the shards - stored data now belongs to one version, a put that names another version's data is refused, and a gateway asks the reference index before removing shards; entries that could not be stored stopped the state machine on every replica - they are now refused (keys, IDs, dates past the year 9999). Also: a repeated `<Rule>` is malformed (only elements the caller marks may repeat); an entry of schema 1 carries no time and no lock; every removal path asks the lock; lifting a lock is audited; the bucket's default retention has an event. | The checkpoint commit `ccff3a8` was made before this review came back, with the step marked in progress; the fixes are the commit after it. |
| 2026-10-08 | Deferred from that review: lock headers are accepted on every operation and only PutObject reads them (multipart and CopyObject must read or refuse them when they arrive); a repeated lock header takes its first value; no test through HTTP of DeleteObjects with the bypass header from a principal without the permission. | Honest list of what was not done. |
| 2026-10-08 | Step 6.1, multipart uploads (ADR-0012). A part is written by the same write path as an object and recorded by its own Raft entry; completing copies nothing: one entry makes a version whose data ID is the upload ID and whose parts are kept beside it, keyed by offset. Whether the listed parts make an object is `object.CheckCompletion`, called by the use case and, with authority, by the state machine. | Nothing is acknowledged before it is durable and committed; a ranged read of a large object reads only the part records it needs. |
| 2026-10-08 | Keys of a multipart object (ADR-0010): one KMS data key per upload, bound to the upload ID, which encrypts nothing; the key of each part is HKDF-SHA256 of it with the part's number and data ID. The place of every part in the completed object is sealed with HMAC-SHA256 under a second derived key and verified on read. | No key encrypts two streams; no KMS call per part. The seal is the stricter reading of "reordering and truncation are detected" for an object of several streams; flagged for Albie in the report. |
| 2026-10-08 | The `data` index is the set of every ID in use - data of versions, data of parts, uploads and the versions they became - and an entry that proposes a used ID for something new is refused. | Extends "stored data belongs to one version" (review of 6.4) to parts, so that no upload entry can take the data of a locked version. |
| 2026-10-08 | An entry that makes parts unreferenced names up to 1000 of them in its answer, and the proposing gateway removes their shards at once; the rest is left to the collector. | The collector's grace period is a day; a deleted multipart object should give its space back at once. An answer travels from the leader to the proposer and must stay small. |
| 2026-10-08 | Deleting a bucket removes its uploads in progress. Uploads of one key are listed in order of ID. Lock headers are read on CreateMultipartUpload and refused on the other multipart requests. CompleteMultipartUpload needs a body covered by the signature. Closes the deferral of the 6.4 review about lock headers on multipart. |
| 2026-10-08 | The multipart entity tag is computed in the S3 adapter from the entity tags the client lists; the use case and the state machine check that those are the stored ones. | MD5 is S3's protocol and stays in the one file where it is allowed. |

| 2026-10-08 | `domain.ErrEntityTooLarge` has a wire code; `domain.ErrConflict` maps to `409 OperationAborted` in the S3 adapter. | The first was lost between the metadata leader and a follower that proposed; the second was answered as a 500. |

SSE-KMS is the deployment's one KMS key asked for by name, with the encryption context stored canonically and bound, with the mode, into what the key is wrapped under. SSE-C encrypts under HKDF(customer key, salted with the KMS data key): it takes both. | 2026-10-08 | Anonymous access: a request without credentials (GET or HEAD only) is the principal nobody, and the authorizer allows it only actions from a fixed list of reads that a `Principal: "*"` statement allows. A bucket policy that cannot be read or parsed refuses the request. | Stricter than S3, which allows world-writable buckets; flagged for Albie. Fail closed, because the policy might have denied. |

| 2026-10-10 | Step 6.9. A copy streams: `ObjectService.Copy` opens the source with `get` and writes it with `put`, so every rule of reading (permissions, customer key, seals, integrity) and of writing (permissions for tags and locks, encryption, quorum, Raft) holds without a second implementation. Nothing is shared between source and copy - a server-side "reference copy" would break "stored data belongs to one version". The entity tag of a copy is the digest of its bytes, also when the source was made of parts. | No new design record: no new state, no new entry. |

| 2026-10-11 | Goal A.5 (Albie). Bucket usage is counted when it is asked for, by walking the bucket's index of current versions, bounded (`complete: false` beyond the bound): it adds no state, so no entry or on-disk schema changes. It costs a walk per bucket card; counters kept by the state machine are the better answer for very large buckets and belong with the schema-3 work of goal B. It is its own endpoint, not fields of the bucket list, so that listing buckets stays cheap and a caller who may list buckets but not a bucket's keys learns no figure. Deleting several objects is the console making one DELETE an object: the API has no bulk call, authorization and Object Lock are decided per object as before, and a refusal stops the run with what was deleted staying deleted. Built-in roles and policies stay read-only (the domain refuses changes to them); the role screen says so and points to making a role of one's own. | No new state for a figure that can be derived; no logic in the console. |

| 2026-10-11 | Goal A.6. Albie: "i want to use the same public license sqlite has." SQLite has no licence: its authors dedicated it to the public domain, with a blessing in place of a legal notice. `LICENSE.md` does the same for CactusFS in its own words, and adds three things SQLite also deals with, elsewhere: what is not covered (the dependencies compiled into a binary keep their licences, and the dedication is of copyright, not of the name and logo), that contributors dedicate their work likewise, and a grant of every permission for jurisdictions that do not recognise a dedication (SQLite sells a licence for those; a free grant serves the same end). This closes one of the two questions open in CLAUDE.md section 11 and with it one of the two things step 7.9 waited for; the module path is still open. Not legal advice: a lawyer should read the file before a release relies on it. | The owner's decision; said in a file a reader can rely on. |

| 2026-10-11 | Goals B, C and D share entry schema 3. A schema-2 node refuses a bucket-config kind it does not know while a newer node accepts it, so a new kind is a change of schema like a new command; and a build that knew only B's commands and reached level 3 would stop on C's. One fixture, one level, one rolling-upgrade test. | 2026-10-11 | B, decided under the stricter reading: downloads are counted in memory on each gateway and flushed through Raft every 30 s (a crash loses up to that; the API calls the figures best-effort, and they are not an audit trail); never on the path of a GET, which must not fail or wait for bookkeeping; who uploaded and who read sit behind a new action `admin:GetObjectAccess` that only `cluster-admin` and `auditor` hold - `s3:GetObject` does not imply it; a presigned read is recorded as the signer's, marked presigned; anonymous is one row; no address is kept; each ranged GET and an aborted transfer count; a copy's source is counted apart. Counts are per version: an overwrite in a bucket without versions starts at zero, and deleting the object removes its record. | A list of who read what is sensitive, and metadata is not encrypted at rest. |
| 2026-10-11 | C, search: plain text is a case-insensitive substring of bucket names and keys; `*` or `?` anywhere makes it a wildcard over the whole name; `rx:` is a Go regular expression (RE2: time linear in the input, no backtracking), case as written. Only buckets the caller may list are searched, each decided by the authorizer, bucket policy included; filtering uses a decision that writes no audit record, or one search over N buckets would write N denials. The walk is bounded (keys examined, buckets, two seconds, matches) and done in chunks so that the metadata lock is let go between them; `complete: false` and a token continue it, and the token grants nothing - every page is authorized again. | A search must not become a way round policies, a flood in the audit trail, or a stall of the write path. |
| 2026-10-11 | C, sub-buckets: a sub-bucket is a named first-level prefix of its bucket, registered with it, at most ten, one level deep, with a policy of its own in the bucket-policy language confined to that prefix. S3 clients address it as `s3://bucket/sub/key`, unchanged; it is a range of keys in the index and no path. The single authorizer judges identity policies, the bucket's policy and the sub-bucket's together: a deny in any refuses, an allow in any allows. Stricter readings, flagged for Albie: a sub-bucket's policy never opens anything to anonymous callers and cannot grant governance bypass; "each bucket must be controlled by policies" is read as "a policy can be attached to every bucket and sub-bucket and edited in the console" - without one, default deny already holds, and requiring one would break S3 `CreateBucket`. A sub-bucket is deleted only when empty. | Child buckets with names of their own would break S3 naming; prefixes with conditions give nothing to manage. |

S3 keeps the case of tags, and two entries of the compatibility baseline assert it (`test_set_bucket_tagging`, `test_put_max_kvsize_tags`); CLAUDE.md section 5 allows no regression there. So lower-casing is a setting of a bucket's tag rules: on for every bucket made through the management API or the console, off for one made with S3 `CreateBucket` until switched on; the catalogue is always lower case. Flagged for Albie: deployment-wide lower-casing is his to choose, at the price of those two baseline entries. Rules name the tag keys an upload must carry (optionally the values allowed) and are enforced in the use case for every way an object gets tags, S3 included; a violation is `InvalidTag`. Tags outside the catalogue stay allowed unless a bucket says otherwise. | His words, kept as far as the compatibility contract he also set allows. |

| 2026-10-12 | Step S.3, what the ADRs leave open. (1) Only the five new commands are refused by the decoder below schema 3. `created_by`, `tags` on `upload.create` and a `bucket.config.put` of kind `tagrules` in an entry that claims schema 2 are not: the first two are dropped when the entry is applied and the third is refused there as a kind there is not - each exactly what the schema-2 binary does with the same bytes, so the two never part; a decoder that stopped on them would stop this release where the last one goes on. `bucket.ValidConfigKind` is unchanged; the state machine allows `tagrules` by the schema byte of the entry, and `versionOf` makes such an entry schema 3, so the level rule refuses it earlier. Payloads of the new commands beside an old command in an older entry are not seen, as that binary does not see them. (2) Who made a version travels in `object.Version.CreatedBy` and `ports.Completion.CreatedBy`; there is no `PutOptions` type, since the domain type already has the field and `PutVersion` takes the version. Below level 3 the store proposes the entry without it (`heldTo`) and the answer says what was stored. Tags of an upload are a new field `object.Upload.Tags`. (3) A repeated batch of reads is one with the node's boot number and a sequence not above the last applied; another boot number begins again. An empty batch counts as a batch. A batch with a node ID that is none, or more than 256 items, is refused whole; any other bad item is skipped, a delete marker included. Removing a node drops its record whether it had registered or not. (4) The record is `{"v":1,...}`; one of another version stops the state machine (`ErrUnsupportedFormat`) rather than being guessed at. Its place is that of the version in `versionids`, so `dropData`, through which every removal of a version goes, removes it. (5) Putting a sub-bucket or a tag definition that exists replaces it and keeps its first `created_at`. A sub-bucket's policy is bounded by `iam.MaxPolicyBytes` (20 KiB), the bound bucket policies are parsed with, not by the 64 KiB of a configuration document. New sentinels with wire codes: `ErrSubBucketNotFound`, `ErrTooManySubBuckets`, `ErrSubBucketNotEmpty`; a missing tag definition is `ErrEntityNotFound`, a 201st `ErrInvalidArgument`. (6) The state machine holds a definition to its bounds in bytes and to no duplicate values; lower case is checked by the proposer (`tagging.Definition.Validate`), because what lower case is depends on the Unicode tables a binary was built with and replicas of two builds must agree. For the same reason `tag.definition.delete` reads the keys of required tags out of a `tagrules` document by itself, exactly as written, and not with `bucket.ParseTagRules`; a document it cannot read names nothing. (7) No event is published for sub-buckets, definitions or reads (none is in the AsyncAPI contract; S.2's contracts are still to do); the audit record of a change is the only one, and a refused change is not audited by the store, as for identities. (8) `MemMetadata` takes an optional `Audit ports.AuditLog` for those records; the shared assertions are `porttest.Schema3Contract`, run against the memory store, a store on its own and a non-voting node of a four-node cluster. Verified on the host only (build, vet, gofumpt, `go test -race ./internal/...`, the three fixture tests); lint, the other gates and `make test-chaos` have not run, so S.3 stays `in_progress`. | The ADRs say what is kept; these are the choices that decide whether two releases, or two replicas, can ever disagree about an entry. |
| 2026-10-12 | Step S.3, fixes after review (3 majors, 6 minors). (1) **A proposer behind its leader.** The leader refuses an entry above the cluster's level with an error that wraps `ErrUnavailable` *and* `ErrUnsupportedFormat` (`aboveLevel`): the pair is how the proposer knows it from every other refusal after it crossed a node boundary, where only wire codes arrive - no new code, no change of the node RPC contract. `Store.propose` then catches up (`consistent`), recomputes the level, never tries the refused schema again (`limit = refused - 1`), re-runs `heldTo`/`versionOf` and proposes again: a put or completion goes without `created_by` and succeeds, everything else is a definite `ErrUnavailable` and only that. The loop is bounded by the number of schemas rather than by "once", so a leader two levels down is handled the same way. (2) **A read is of the version that was read.** `object.reads.add` items carry `version_id`, the version's own ID (null versions have one), beside the name; `ports.ObjectRead.VersionID`. Apply skips an item whose named version has another ID, or that carries none: stricter than accepting old-shape items, and schema 3 was never written. (3) **Upload tags in apply** are bounded by 10 tags, a key of 1..512 bytes and a value of at most 1024 bytes (`maxUploadTags`, `maxUploadTagKeyBytes`, `maxUploadTagValueBytes`: four bytes a character of S3's 128/256), fixed in `raftmeta` as part of the replicated format; the character check is `Store.CreateUpload`'s, before proposing. The two schema-2 sites that still call `object.ValidateTags` in apply are left and named in ADR-0016 "Known debt". (4) A batch of reads is its node's: new cluster action `cluster:ReportReads`, `Store.Subject` returns the batch's node as a third value, and node RPC asks the authorizer as it does for `node.set`/`node.remove`; the state machine applies a batch of a node that is not registered as nothing and does not remember it, when the state has any node at all (a node on its own registers none). (5) Each count of a read is at most 2^32 (`object.MaxReadCount`) and sums saturate at MaxInt64; the fold rule and the stored bytes are pinned by `testdata/access-record.golden.json`. (6) What apply would refuse without looking at the state is refused by the `Store` method before proposing (sub-bucket policy size; tag rules over 64 KiB; for an upload with tags its tags, key, ID and key mode), so a refused call does not raise the state to schema 3. Not covered: a put or completion with `created_by` that apply refuses for another reason (an invalid key, an ID in use) still raises it - the use cases validate those first. | A red test, a non-retryable error where the ports promise "unavailable", a reader credited to the wrong object, and a refusal that depended on the Go version: all of them in the replicated format, so they had to be right before schema 3 is written once. |
| 2026-10-12 | Steps B.2-B.5, what ADR-0016 leaves open. (1) **One counted path.** The exported `ObjectService.Get` is the download: it wraps the body it hands out, and the read is counted once when that body is closed, however often and however far it was read. Copies read through the unexported `get` and are counted by `Copy` and `CopyPart` after their commit; the healer, the scrubber, the collector and the lifecycle worker never call `Get`, and a table test runs each of them over a service that counts and finds nothing counted. (2) **UploadPartCopy** counts one copy for every part that is committed, not one for the upload: the part's commit is the only commit the use case sees, and it is the rule of ranged GETs, each counted by itself. (3) **The reader** is `actorOf(principal)`, the audit trail's function. `iam.Session.Presigned` is new and set by the S3 handler where a signature in the URL was verified; an anonymous principal is never marked presigned; node and service principals, delete markers and versions without an ID are not recorded. (4) **The bound** of 10 000 counts the aggregates held and those of the batch that waits for its outcome; a read of an aggregate already held still counts beyond it; `cactusfs_object_access_dropped_total` counts reads (downloads and copies), not aggregates; a count that would pass `object.MaxReadCount` between two flushes is dropped too, since the store would refuse the item. (5) **A batch refused as invalid** (`ErrInvalidArgument`, and not "outcome unknown") is dropped and counted instead of sent again: it would be refused every time and hold up everything counted after it. It cannot happen with the batches the recorder builds; every other failure keeps the batch and its number. (6) The recorder runs on every node, flushes only when `raftmeta.Store.Check` passes (a leader is known; background work starts after the node has registered), gives a batch 30 s and the flush of a stop 10 s, and says once, at warn, when flushing begins to fail. `--access-flush-interval` below 5 s is refused, 0 included: counting cannot be switched off. (7) **The API.** `ObjectService.Access(ctx, principal, bucket, key, versionID string)` reads the version name itself, after the authorizer was asked, so that who may not see the record learns nothing from a 400 or a 404 either; a delete marker named as the version is `ErrMethodNotAllowed`. The management API had no answer for `ErrVersionNotFound` and `ErrMethodNotAllowed` (500): they are now 404 `NoSuchVersion` and 405 `MethodNotAllowed`. `last_downloaded_at` is the last download or copy; `readers` is always an array. (8) Telemetry: a decorator of its own, `InstrumentObjectAccess` (port `object_access`), rather than a wider `telemetry.Metadata`; `ObserveAccessDropped`. (9) `test/clustertest` gained `HTTP(id)` and `Admin(id)`, HTTP clients for a node's S3 and admin listeners, for the presigned GET and the management API of `TestObjectAccessAcrossTheCluster`. (10) Console: the access figures are a component of their own under "Object details", loaded by itself, so that a refusal is one muted line and never the alert of the screen; another failure is a muted line with its reason. (11) Threat model: a row under information disclosure. (12) **For Albie:** every put, copy and completion now carries `created_by`, an entry of schema 3 - so once every member knows schema 3, the first upload makes the state schema 3 and the release before can no longer open it ("until first use" in ADR-0016 is, in practice, until the first upload). Verified on the host only: build, vet (all tags), gofumpt, `go test -race ./internal/... ./cmd/...`, coverage, console lint and typecheck. | 2026-10-12 | Steps C.5 and the search parts of C.2, C.6 and C.8, built per bucket before sub-buckets exist; what the plan leaves open. (1) **Order and token.** Buckets are walked in name order and each is done whole: its name (a result only with `s3:ListAllMyBuckets`), then its keys. The token is base64url of `{v:1, q, b, k, n}` where `n` says that the name of bucket `b` itself was looked at and `k` is the last key examined, carried as bytes; a bucket that is gone is resumed at the next name. A 1024-byte key does not fit the 1024 characters first meant for `continuation`, so the contract allows 4096. (2) **Scope.** `Authorizer.Allowed` filters; nothing else does. A search that names a `bucket` is gated by `Authorize` (`s3:ListBucket`) before the bucket is looked for, so who may not list it gets 403 whether it exists or not; its name is then no result, and a token of another bucket is refused. Who may see nothing from the token's place on is checked against the buckets before it too, and only when none is theirs and they may not list buckets are they refused, once, through `Authorize` (`s3:ListAllMyBuckets`). Sub-bucket scope plugs in at one marked place, `searchWalk.mayList`. (3) **Bounds.** They are checked only at a bucket the caller may see, and only such buckets count towards the 200: a stop, and so a token anybody can decode, never names another bucket, and the number of pages says nothing of the buckets passed over. The price: every page asks the authorizer about each bucket it passes, which reads that bucket's policy, and the two seconds do not cover that - linear in the number of buckets; a cache of bucket policies in the authorizer is the remedy if deployments grow that way. Every request does at least one walk, so it gets further than the one before even when the clock is already past. A walk copies key, size, time and ETag and matches after the store's lock is let go; it reads one key beyond its chunk to learn that there is more. (4) **Failures.** A policy that cannot be read fails the search (503) rather than pass part of an answer off as the whole; a stored policy that is damaged leaves its bucket out, as a listing of it is refused, and the authorizer logs it. A bucket deleted between the listing and the walk is skipped. (5) **429.** New sentinel `domain.ErrTooManyRequests`, wire code `too_many_requests`, answered 429 `TooManyRequests` with `Retry-After: 1` and a detail; `ErrTooManyAttempts` keeps its 300 s. A place is taken after the query, limit and token are validated and before any store is read, by the audit trail's name of the principal: two keys of one user share a place, as do all anonymous callers. Two principals can hold both places of a node for about two seconds a request - search is then refused to others, nothing else is. (6) **API.** Tag `search`, `x-cactusfs-action: s3:ListBucket`; `limit=0` is 400 at the API while zero in the use case means the default; the bounds can be made small only through `export_test.go`. `porttest.Authorizer` keeps what `Allowed` was asked apart (`Filtered`). There is no telemetry decorator of the authorizer; the search reads through the instrumented metadata ports and adds no metric. A request without `q` is answered 400 before it is authenticated, as one without `key` already is: the generated router binds parameters first. (7) **Console.** The field is always in the page; folded, the stylesheet hides it and shows a magnifier that unfolds the navigation and focuses the field; on a phone it is a row of its own under the logo. The screen says how the API read the query (`match`), offers "Search again" when the first page failed, shows "Open folder" only for a key that has one, and keeps what was found when a further page is refused. Not verified here: `make check` (no containers on this host), golangci-lint, and the console tests, which do not start on this host (the bundler's native binding is missing) - so C.5 stays `in_progress`. | The owner's words were "all objects allowed by the policies": the authorizer decides every bucket on every page, and nothing the caller can forge or count widens that. |
| 2026-10-12 | Search, after review: what the row above says of the cost of deciding buckets, of the two places of a node and of where bounds are checked no longer holds. (1) **One reading of the policies a page.** New port `ports.BucketPolicies` (`BucketPolicies(ctx) map[name]document`): the policy of every bucket from this node's own copy of the state, in one read transaction, without asking the leader; implemented by `raftmeta.LocalConfig` and `porttest.MemMetadata`, wired in `cmd/cactusfs/node.go`. A search lists the buckets (linearizable, as before) and then tells the request scope where the policies are (`primeBucketPolicies`, unexported in `internal/app`); the authorizer's `scopedPolicy` takes a bucket's policy from that one reading, made the first time a request that a bucket policy binds is decided - root causes none - and `decide` is unchanged and still the one decision behind `Authorize` and `Allowed`. A node's applied state never goes back, so after the listing the local copy is at least as new as the listing. A search of one named `bucket` is gated as before, by one consistent read of that bucket's policy. (2) **All buckets are decided before the token is looked at** (`searchWalk.visible`): 1 + N decisions a page for N buckets, whoever asks and whatever the token says; the walk is then given only the buckets the caller may see and positioned among them, so bounds, `examined`, `complete`, the number of pages and every token speak of those alone (decision 3 of the row above is kept, by construction now). The token's `b` must be a valid bucket name (`bucket.ValidateName`), else 400 before anything is read: a name and that name with a byte after it cannot be told apart by cost. (3) **Time.** Deciding counts towards the two seconds, measured from when deciding begins, and `ctx` is checked at every bucket. When deciding alone takes the two seconds the request fails with `domain.ErrUnavailable` (503, `Retry-After: 1`, no detail to the caller) instead of stopping with a token: a stop among buckets that are not the caller's must not be named, and a call that went on from one would decide every bucket again and get no further. No refusal is audited for it. When deciding leaves no time, the call still does one walk, as before. (4) **Eight searches a node**, still one a principal: with two, two principals searching without pause refused search to everybody else. (5) **API.** `/search` names its `429` with a `Retry-After` header (additive; the handler answers as before, through the problem writer) and says what a page costs and when it answers 503. (6) **Console.** `useLoad` hands its loader an `AbortSignal`, aborted when the answer is no longer wanted; loaders that ignore it are unchanged. The search screen passes it to the first page and aborts a further page that is on its way when the screen goes or the same query is sent again, which also clears the error of a further page. The fragment's query is read with `decodeURIComponent` pair by pair and built with `encodeURIComponent`, no longer as a form: a `+` typed into the address is a plus, and the console writes a space as `%20`; an address written by an earlier build with `+` for a space (a prefix with a space in it) now reads with the plus - none was released. The field of the navigation follows `q` while the search screen is shown and keeps what was typed elsewhere. Residual, accepted: a page lists the buckets and decides each in memory, so its time grows with the number of buckets in the deployment - the same for every caller, which lets a caller estimate that number but not which names exist; `raftmeta.LocalConfig` is read without a telemetry decorator, as it already was for CORS. Not verified on this host: `make check`, golangci-lint, the console tests (the bundler's native binding is missing) - the new console tests were written without being run. | A page of a search must cost one question to the leader, not one for each bucket, must cost the same whatever the caller may see or forges, and must keep to its time; and it must stay the authorizer that decides. |
| 2026-10-12 | Search, after the second review: what the row above says of one reading of all policies, of `BucketPolicies(ctx) map`, of "no detail to the caller" for the 503 and of `LocalConfig` being read without telemetry no longer holds. (1) **The port pages.** `ports.BucketPolicies` is `BucketPolicies(ctx, from string, visit func(name string, document []byte) error) (next string, err error)`: one page of buckets in name order from the first not before `from`, `visit` for each that has a policy, `next` the first bucket not read or empty at the end; `ctx` checked at every bucket and before every visit; the document is lent for the call. `raftmeta.LocalConfig` reads a page in one read transaction of at most 256 buckets or 1 MiB of stored records and calls `visit` after the transaction has ended - so the adapter holds one bounded page of decoded documents, not nothing: the alternative, visiting inside the transaction, would have held the state's read lock while a policy is hashed and, cold, parsed (half a millisecond for 16 KiB). Measured (`BenchmarkLocalBucketPolicies`, Apple M3): 1.2-1.6 ms a page of 45 buckets with 17 KiB policies, 0.7-0.9 ms a page of 256 with 1.2 KiB. `porttest.MemMetadata` pages by three buckets (`PolicyPage`) and overwrites a document once `visit` has returned, so a test finds whoever keeps one. (2) **Parsed policies are remembered** by the `Authorizer` (`policyCache`, a field of the value the composition root builds; mutex; no package state): by bucket name, under the SHA-256 of the document, asked only with the hash of bytes just read; a document that does not parse is never stored. Bounds: 131 072 entries and 128 MiB of documents - a parsed policy takes 1.6 bytes of heap for a byte of document, so about 200 MiB - chosen to hold 7 800 policies of 16 KiB or 118 000 of 1 KiB without letting whoever may put bucket policies take more memory of every node than that; what finds no room is not stored and is parsed by every search. Eviction: every walk is numbered, an entry carries the newest walk that had begun when it was last read, and a walk that read all buckets from the first to the last drops what no walk has come by since it began (looked through only when it found fewer than there are). Only a primed scope - a search of all buckets - uses it; any other request, a search of one named bucket included, does its one linearizable `GetBucketConfig` and parses what it read (tested by read counts). (3) **A request keeps one page**: the scope's `policyWalk` holds the page it read last and the search one bit a bucket; `scope.policies` stays empty in a search. The scope expects questions in name order and, asked about a bucket behind its page, begins again there. Because a decision now reads at most a page, the time of a call is looked at between pages and not only after one reading of everything. (4) **503.** New sentinel `domain.ErrTooManyBuckets` (wire code `too_many_buckets`), returned together with `ErrUnavailable` when deciding takes the time of a call; the admin API gives that one 503 a `detail` in its own words - a limit past a size of the deployment and not an outage, more work just after a restart, not for a named bucket nor for root - and keeps `Retry-After: 1`, since a node that has just started gets further with each request: what it parsed before it gave up is remembered. Measured (`BenchmarkSearchDeciding`, Apple M3, deciding only): 5 000 x 16 KiB cold 2.4-2.6 s and 2.3 GB, warm 37-43 ms and 2.3 MB; 20 000 x 1.1 KiB cold 0.8-1.0 s and 707 MB, warm 23-54 ms and 9 MB. With the reading of the pages the limit is about 10 000 buckets with 16 KiB policies or 150 000 with 1 KiB (it was 4 600 and 55 000), set by the 128 MiB: beyond them a policy is parsed every time. (5) **Read or document.** A page that cannot be read - damage to the store included - is `ErrUnavailable` and fails the search; `ErrIntegrity` reaches `searchWalk.mayList` only for the damaged document of one bucket, which is left out alone. A request that is gone fails as `context.Canceled`. (6) **Telemetry.** `telemetry.InstrumentBucketPolicies` counts and traces every page as `metadata.BucketPolicies`; wired in `cmd/cactusfs/node.go`. A search of 5 000 buckets with large policies is 111 such spans. (7) **Admin API log.** A 5xx of a request whose context the client cancelled is logged at INFO as "request cancelled by the client" with the same fields, not at ERROR as "request failed"; the S3 adapter has no such rule for its "s3 request failed" (it only keeps a cancelled, undelivered response out of the log), so the rule is this adapter's. (8) **Console.** The two search loaders ask once more, after `Retry-After` seconds (1 when absent, 3 at the most), when the answer is 429 with the code `TooManyRequests`, and honour the abort signal while they wait; nothing is shown of the first refusal. (9) C.7 is `in_progress`: the search is wired, sub-buckets are not. Not verified on this host: `make check`, golangci-lint, the console tests (the bundler's native binding is missing; the new ones use fake timers and were written without being run). Open for Albie: whether the 128 MiB should be a setting, and a quota on buckets (milestone 8) as the remedy for the limit. | A page of a search must not parse every policy of the deployment again, must not hold them all, and must not hold the metadata lock for the length of the reading; and where a limit remains it must be said to be one. |
