| 2026-10-12 | 7.7, 7.8 as built, and the two things 7.4 left. (1) **Node RPC** is traced and counted by four interceptors of the telemetry adapter written with the core OpenTelemetry API (`otelgrpc` is no dependency and stays none): a span a call on each side, `cactusfs.noderpc/<Method>`, the caller's trace in the gRPC metadata as a W3C `traceparent`, with the `tracestate` beside it when there is one - the protobuf schema is untouched; `cactusfs_noderpc_requests_total{side,method,code}` and `..._request_duration_seconds{side,method}`. `method` is one of the methods of the generated service descriptors or `other`, `code` one of gRPC's seventeen (`Unknown` for a number a peer made up). Without a collector nothing is read from or written to the metadata. A stream that gRPC reports as `Canceled`, and on which no send and no receive had failed, is counted `OK` on the calling side, whoever cancelled it: that is how a shard writer ends its stream after the commit was confirmed, and a stream ended because its request's context was cancelled before any message failed looks the same from there and is counted `OK` too; the serving side tells a commit from an abandoned write. (2) **One rule for `result`**, kept: `error` whenever a port returns an error, a missing user or session and a refused password included; the IAM, session and idempotency ports are therefore in no alert, as the metadata port is in none. (3) **A port without its decorator is found by looking at what was built**: a test walks a started node by reflection - the node, its use cases, what they hold - and every field that is an interface of `internal/ports`, or any interface holding a value of `internal/adapters`, must hold a `telemetry.Instrumented` (an interface whose one method cannot be written outside that package) or be of a kind listed in the test with its reason: the clock, the authorizer, the erasure codec, the composition of drives, shard readers, readiness checks. Its first run found one: the bucket service read this node's copy of a bucket's configuration undecorated (`local_config` now). The view of the cluster and the identity provider are decorated as well. The password hasher has no context and so no span: counted and timed only. (4) **Gauges are read at scrape time** through four small interfaces of the telemetry adapter that the composition root implements from accessors - `raftmeta.Store.Observe` (what Raft keeps in memory, up to three short reads of the state under the state machine's lock; it never waits for `Close`, and can wait for that lock), `disk.Drive.Usage` (one `fstatfs` through the drive's handle, `GetDiskFreeSpaceEx` by name on Windows) - with no goroutine that polls; every source is asked through a bounded probe, see the row of the review below. A gauge whose source is absent is not exposed. `cactusfs_metadata_schema_supported` was added beside `..._schema_level`: an unfinished upgrade is a binary that supports more than the cluster writes, which the level alone cannot show. (5) **Certificates and handshakes, as far as `nodetls` reaches today**: the certificate's end is read from the configuration the identity hands a listener; handshakes are counted on the node RPC port by wrapping the server's transport credentials, and on the Raft port only where the listener asks the composition root - a node that is not admitted. A handshake the Raft listener itself fails is not counted until `nodetls` reports it (7.5; marked in `cmd/cactusfs/cluster.go`). (6) **Twelve alerts** on the new metrics with their tests, a group for the metadata group, the drives, the nodes and the audit trail; panels on two dashboards; the list of metrics in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, held to the registry in both directions with types and labels. (7) **A PodMonitor replaces the ServiceMonitor** (`podMonitor.*`): it selects the pods, so the nodes that are no gateway are scraped and no Service and no NetworkPolicy changes; both would have scraped the gateways twice. A pod is scraped before it is ready, and a node that is starting refuses `/metrics` with everything else: it shows as not answering. Not done: a gauge of the scrubber's last pass and a count of refusals while starting (no alert for either); `make check` was not run in the session that wrote this (no container engine there) - `promtool`, `helm`, `yq` and `golangci-lint` have not seen these files, so 7.7 and 7.8 stay `in_progress` until they have. | What is observed must be what was built, not what the wiring was meant to be; a label or an attribute takes no value somebody else chooses. |
| 2026-10-12 | Review of 7.7 and 7.8 (2 major, 7 minor), and what is guaranteed now. (1) **A scrape is not held by a source, whatever the source does.** `Observe` and `Check` of the metadata store read under the lock of the state machine, which an entry holds while it is applied and a snapshot for as long as it is restored, and `Check` does not look at its context: "no source may wait" was a wish. Every source of the state gauges - Raft and metadata, drives, certificate, readiness - is now asked through one kind of probe in `telemetry/state.go`: all are asked first and waited for against one deadline (`Config.StateTimeout`, two seconds), so a scrape is held two seconds at most, not two a source; the series of a source that has not answered are absent from that scrape, not zero; one question is out to a source at a time, so a source that hangs costs one goroutine and is asked again only after it has answered. Kept, against the letter of the finding: `cactusfs_drive_online` is 0 for every drive the last answer named when the drives do not answer - a drive that does not answer cannot be reached, which is what the gauge says and what `CactusFSDriveOffline` reads; its room is absent. Not done, on purpose: no atomic copy of the level or of the state's schema beside the state. The level is worked out from the members' records and Raft's configuration under the same lock, so a copy of the schema alone would leave the lock where it is, and a copy of the level would have to be kept right in `Apply`, `Restore` and every change of membership; and a `Check` that did not take the lock would call a node ready while it restores a snapshot, which `/readyz` does not do today. `/readyz` itself still waits for that lock - `Check` does not look at the deadline the handler gives it; that is older than this change, and open. (2) **`CactusFSNoMetadataLeader` asks two questions**: no node that reports leads, and no node that reports is ready (`max(cactusfs_raft_is_leader) == 0 and on (job) max(cactusfs_ready) == 0`). A node is ready only while it knows of a leader, so a leader whose series are missing - unreachable, or scraped without its gauges - is vouched for by its followers. Chosen over comparing the number of series with the number of targets, which would have been silent whenever any node is down - a lost majority included, the commonest way to have no leader. `CactusFSMetadataLeaderUnstable` is above five changes of term an hour (a rolling restart of five voters makes five); `CactusFSAuditLogErrors` reads `method="AppendAudit"` only. `promtool` has not run these: the arithmetic is in the comments of the tests. (3) **`disk.Drive.Usage` asks through the drive's `os.Root`**: the drive file by `Lstat` on the root, the room by `fstatfs` on the directory opened through it (an `os.Root` gives out no descriptor); on Windows the room is still asked by name, since `GetDiskFreeSpaceEx` takes one. A drive that is closed now answers with an error where it answered with the room of its path. (4) The test of the state gauges matched `d1` in a label value that is a random ID and failed one run in five; it now holds the `drive` label to the generated IDs, whole. The comments on streams counted `OK` and on `tracestate`, and the blind spots of the wiring test (`Leader: n.meta.Leader`, `Ready: n.meta.Check`, `Active: n.meta.IsLeader`: functions, not ports, undecorated), say what the code does. `make check` has still not run on this change; 7.7 and 7.8 stay `in_progress`. | A comment that promises what the code cannot keep is worse than none; an alert must not take a missing answer for a no. |

Dialling is counted too - a node that answers on a Raft address with a certificate of another CA or another node - which node RPC's client side is not. (5) **A count read from elsewhere never goes back**: `tally` in `telemetry/state.go` keeps the total and the last reading of a source, and a source that is exchanged for one that starts at nothing adds to it. In the contract `reason` is a closed set, because the answering node makes it; `kind`, `scope`, `source` and a certificate's `use` are open strings, because they pass through from a node that may be newer - an unknown kind is treated as a secret. Of a node only `node_id` and `answered` are required. Why a node did not answer is a reason and never its error's words: `unsupported` (the call is not known to it), `timeout`, `unreachable`, `failed`; the cause is logged where it happened. What a node may send is bounded by sender and receiver - 512 settings, 256 drives, 16 certificates, 16 labels and roles, 4 096 bytes a value - and the receiver redacts again. | 2026-10-12 | Goal H, review findings fixed (statuses unchanged). (1) **Volumes, concurrent callers**: `volumeProbe` of `cmd/cactusfs/localsettings.go` answered "not reached" at once to whoever asked while a question was out, so two consoles or two gateways at the same moment saw healthy drives offline. It is a single-flight now: a later caller waits for the outstanding answer against its own two seconds; a hung volume is asked once, costs one goroutine and each caller its deadline. The telemetry state probe did not have that flaw (a second scrape already waited for the question that was out), but concurrent scrapes inflated `cactusfs_tls_reload_refused_total`: two scrapes holding answers of different age applied them in either order and the older reading was taken for a source that began again. Questions are numbered and a reading is counted once, never after a later one. (2) **OTLP endpoint**: a URL with `@`, `?` or `#` is refused at start - the characters `cluster.RedactURL` takes for a credential, so what is used is what is shown - and every refusal names the redacted URL. The exporter never sent userinfo or a query (it takes host and path), so no credential in the URL ever worked; CactusFS has no setting for one. `telemetry.New` installs OpenTelemetry's process-wide error handler when it exports (`telemetry.ExportErrors`): WARN, a fixed message, the class and Go type of the error, never its text, one line a minute. A step never ends on such a key: it counts it and goes on, past `limit` if it must, to the next key that can be a cursor, and ends the keys when none follows - so "at or before the cursor, byte for byte" stays true of every key. | 2026-10-13 | Goal L, after the first review of L.0 to L.8 (ADR-0027, revised). (1) A locked session is given no stored answer and reserves no idempotency key: `IdempotencyService.Begin` is told the principal and whether the request is an upload, and for a locked principal and anything else asks the authorizer (`auth:RepeatRequest`) before it looks for anything - 423, audited, no entry written. An upload keeps its idempotency. (2) The keeper of upload leases writes at every tick while an upload is in flight or one began or ended since, and compares nothing with the tick; `UploadBegun` wakes it when the session's lease is not there or lasts less than two thirds of a grace; a lease's expiry is said at the write; a pass has one deadline. (3) A change of password ends every session of the user, active or locked, on every gateway: derived, as the lock is, from a mark among the sessions - `password-change:<hash of the user>`, written with `session.put` after the password, kept 48 h - against which a session whose `CreatedAt` is not later has ended. (4) The node that began a session deletes its record (`session.delete`) a tick after it has ended, and its lapsed lease; the leader's sweep keeps its horizon of `session-lock-max` for what nodes forgot. (5) Only the unlock that itself ends a session - the fifth wrong password - clears the cookie; an unlock that finds its session ended answers 401 and leaves it. The use case tells the two apart by naming `ErrTooManyAttempts` beside `ErrCredentialNotFound`. (6) L.8b is written (`TestRollingUpgradeFromSchema3ToSchema4SessionLock`); it has not run: it needs the container harness. | (1) A replayed answer is not authorized again (ADR-0007), which let a locked session read the secret of an API key it made while active. In the use case, not the handler; refused before reserving, so that a locked request does not cost two entries first. (2) On a real clock a tick a microsecond early was skipped by `now-written >= grace/3`: the cadence was two thirds of a grace, and an upload that began under a lease about to lapse had none for up to a tick - the session read as ended and the console gave its uploads up. (3) The owner's ruling by the stricter reading of CLAUDE.md §3. The store cannot list or delete the sessions of a user, and `raftmeta` was not to be touched: no command, no field, no file there changes. Costs one reading more per session request. Not honoured by a member of a previous release (the session lives to its timeout there, as before); a session begun through a gateway whose clock is behind, within that difference after the change, is ended too. OPEN FOR ALBIE: whether a mark among the sessions is acceptable, as the lease was; the alternative is a residual risk with the remedy "disable the user". (4) Kept for `session-lock-max` past its timeout, every sign-in's record made the state some 25 times what it was. A leader-side removal needs the sessions listed, which the port and `raftmeta` cannot do: left for a step that may touch them. (5) The request may be the unlock itself, sent twice: by then the browser holds the new cookie. (6) Sessions are what they are since ADR-0006, so the test holds for whichever release before the image is of; with `make test-rolling SCHEMA3_COMMIT=84334a7` only this test is expected to pass (the two others assert the level of schema 3). |

| 2026-10-15 | Goal L, after the second review (1 blocker, 4 major, 9 minor - all met; the console's part unrun, vitest does not start where it was written). (1) PLAN L.8 said L.8b was not written; it is (`session_lock_rolling_test.go`), and has not run. (2) A session is dated when its password is about to be checked (`SignIn` reads the clock before `IdentityProvider.Authenticate`), so a sign-in with the old password whose Argon2id check straddles the Raft commit of a change is ended by the mark; an unlock that straddles one keeps the old `CreatedAt` and its renewed session is ended too. (3) The mark is held with a margin: a session not later than mark + `PasswordChangeMargin` (5 s) is ended - a session from before the change through a gateway up to 5 s ahead is ended, more than 5 s ahead survives; a sign-in with the new password within 5 s of the change is ended and its user signs in again. (4) A mark that cannot be written after the password is audited (`auth:EndSessions`, failed, reason and the store's error, never the password); one that cannot be read refuses the request. (5) Session, lease and mark stay three reads: `ports.SessionStore` reads one record at a time and one consistent read needs a port method and `raftmeta`, which Goal L does not touch - the cost is recorded in ADR-0027. (6) Console: an unlock is given up after `UNLOCK_TIMEOUT_MS` = 20 s (aborted; the lock says the server could not be reached); `DELETE /session` never waits for an unlock, and an unlock it abandoned that the API still carries out is ended with its own tokens; every request carries the generation of its tokens, raised when an unlock keeps the new CSRF token, and a 401 of an older generation is not "signed out" - for an upload it is an error of status 0, retried by the engine with the same `Idempotency-Key` and the new tokens. (7) The sign-in notice says why a session ended as far as the console can tell: timed out (a 401 to its own `GET /session` at the timeout or while locked), the password wrong too many times (a 401 to an unlock after `UNLOCK_ATTEMPTS - 1` = 4 wrong ones, counted in the console), otherwise "You were signed out." (8) Tests that kill the surviving mutants P01, P02, N03, N07, N12, N17, N26, N28: `TestAPasswordChangeRaces`, `TestUploadLeaseKeeperPassHasADeadline`, `TestUploadLeaseKeeper` (the write after the last upload, retried), `TestEndedSessionsAreRemoved` (a renewed session), `TestANodeWiresWhatEndsAndRefusesSessions` (`cmd/cactusfs`), and the sub-test (d) of `TestSessionLock` (`test/integration`). (9) ADR-0027 corrected: disabling a user refuses its sessions while it is disabled, re-enabling brings them back; `removeEnded` under clock skew beyond about two thirds of a grace ends a lock early; what the rolling test does not cover and how it is to be named once 8.0.11 selects by exact name (the Makefile is left as it is). | (2) Successful sign-ins are not rate-limited: a thief with the old password could race a reset. (3) The owner's ruling: the stricter side of the trade. (4) The caller's error alone leaves no trace of sessions that outlived a change. (6) A hung unlock held every upload and the sign-out. The generation is the one thing the console knows of which tokens a request carried; status 0 because the API stored nothing under the key and the same request with the new tokens is what the user unlocked for. (7) The API does not say why; the count is a presentation heuristic and decides nothing. |
